## One-way split of the largest round trips, isolated

Each rep's largest round trips (rtt's RTT_ONE_WAY, the hosts' NIC receive timestamps on CLOCK_REALTIME, both on the PTP hardware clock): out is the ping's send to the pong host's NIC, back the pong host's NIC to the ping host's (the pong's host and the way back), the ping's host its NIC to the echo's arrival. The two hosts' clocks may differ by their sync error (timesync logs), which moves between out and back.

## Overlaps against chance (1,000 circular shifts of each rep's round trips, seed 1), isolated

Each rep's round trips shifted together by a random offset within its run (its round trips' sum), so
their spacing and every source's own timing stay; p is the share of shifts overlapping at least as many.

| run | source | of the largest round trips | expected by chance | p |

## Agent loop gaps by what overlapped them, isolated

Every gap of an agent's loop the DPDK event log stamped (modes with @RUSTERON_DPDK_EVENT_LOG), on the
agent's CPU: a traced preemption; else interrupts there (their time inside the gap); else a gap of the
spare CPU's spinner on the same host (the whole VM paused); else nothing the guest saw: the agent's own
work, or the hypervisor taking this vCPU alone. Lengths in µs: median (largest).

| host | class | gaps | length | interrupt time inside | what |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| a | interrupts | 4 | 10.6 (11.4) | 5.0 (5.0) | arch_timer (4) |
| a | VM paused | 3,787 | 15.0 (30.9) | 0.0 (0.0) | spinner gap (3787) |
| a | nothing in the guest | 704 | 11.6 (23.1) | 0.0 (0.0) | - |
| b | interrupts | 4 | 10.4 (11.2) | 5.0 (5.0) | arch_timer (4) |
| b | VM paused | 136 | 11.7 (29.9) | 0.0 (0.0) | spinner gap (136) |
| b | nothing in the guest | 108 | 10.8 (19.4) | 0.0 (0.0) | - |

The spare CPU's gap spinner, each host, the whole state (vm.sh diag-start; its threshold is the
diag log's threshold_us):

| host | gaps | 10-20 µs | 20-50 µs | 50-100 µs | 100 µs or more | largest |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| a | 58,643 | 58,205 | 432 | 0 | 6 | 1298 |
| b | 3,818 | 3,779 | 39 | 0 | 0 | 34 |

Fix: `ethtool -C ens36 adaptive-rx off rx-usecs 0`; ENA turns adaptive moderation back on at every reset, so apply it again after one (when carrier_changes moves) (evidence: git show 128747b9^:io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261015T070339Z/tables.md (c1/c2-base, -nodim, -nocoalesce: lines 22-29, 50-57, 63-70; loop counters lines 386, 598, 1232, 1450; pre-empting worker kworker/2:1, lines 186 and 190): AWS c7i, recv-busy, two 8-minute reps each; io/benches/results/flags-aws-20261019T175248Z/env-rcv.txt (lines 81-82, 146-149: on with rx-usecs 20 as found, back on after a reset); Linux drivers/net/ethernet/amazon/ena/ena_netdev.c (ena_up enables adaptive moderation); aws-graviton-getting-started perfrunbook (adaptive-rx off for latency-sensitive work)) (ERGON_HOST_CHECKS=off silences host-check warnings)"
